› Message from general-purpose
  The Epic 1 context file is written at <repo>/_bmad-
  output/implementation-artifacts/epic-1-context.md. It follows the required
  headings exactly and keeps requirement IDs next to purpose-based descriptions,
  with no section numbers, line numbers or verbatim source copies.

  It is about 1,010 words, which I estimate at roughly 1,360–1,550 tokens, so it
  may sit just over the 1,500-token cap. To get there I cut detail that the
  story specs already carry: exact version pins, the `compose.yaml` settings,
  the Norwegian UI strings and the design-token counts.

  **What it includes**
  - **Goal and stories:** the goal paragraph and the titles of Stories 1.1 to
  1.8.
  - **Requirements:** login, isolation with the two-account test, seed data,
  persistence and safe reset, local/free/offline and robustness (FR-1 to FR-4,
  NFR-1/3/4/6/9/10, DR-4, SM-4). It also condenses the "rules for every story":
  Definition of Done, pull request contents, code review, where logs go, session
  exports and the responsive-view checks.
  - **Technical decisions:** the pure core (AD-1 to AD-3), login and state
  (AD-6, AD-10), data access and weeks (AD-7, AD-8), error pages (AD-12), the
  local run mode and secret file (AD-13, AD-14), seed and reset (AD-15), and the
  single answer key (AD-17).
  - **UX and dependencies:** the UX rules for the app shell and error pages, the
  story order, and the dependencies on Epics 2 to 4.

  **Gaps and conflicts between the sources** (I found no direct contradictions
  on Epic 1 content):
  1. **Week label in Story 1.8's header.** The epics say the header uses
  `forecasting/display.py`, but Story 2.3 builds that module. The fix (1.8
  creates the label with a test, 2.3 extends it) exists only as an open point in
  `.logs/mounia-03/issues.md`, not in `epics.md`. I put it in the file. A
  related gap: the header's latest data week must be derived and never stored
  (AD-8), and nothing outside the core may calculate domain values (AD-2). The
  sources don't say where 1.8 does that derivation before the Epic 2 core
  exists.
  2. **Story 1.6's demo figures are never re-checked.** Story 1.6 promises what
  «Denne uken» will show for Testbedrift A: 4 products to order soon, 31 units,
  1 without a forecast and 4 with warnings. Only Epic 2's core computes these
  figures. No later story checks them; Epic 2 checks only the rule test products
  and warning coverage, and Story 2.8 uses the figures as example values. A
  wrong seed would surface late.
  3. **Missing session secret.** The documented commands run `migrate` before
  `load_seed` creates `.secret_key`, and CI (Story 1.2) runs `pytest` without
  `load_seed`. No source says what the settings use as the secret when the file
  doesn't exist yet.
  4. **Sorting in Story 1.8.** AD-2 says nothing outside the core sorts domain
  values. Story 1.8's temporary list is sorted by product number, before the
  core exists, and the sources don't say where that ordering belongs.
  5. **Header on error pages.** AD-6 exempts error pages from login because they
  show no company data. The UX spec puts the company and latest data week in
  the header on every page after login. The sources don't say which header a
  logged-in user's 404 or 500 page shows; the 503 case is settled.
  6. **Epic 1's "Also covers" list.** It is narrower than the requirement lists
  in its own stories, which also cite NFR-2, NFR-5, NFR-11, DR-1, DR-7, SM-1,
  SM-2, SM-7, AD-3, AD-9, AD-10 and AD-11. This is probably deliberate, since
  other epics own those, but a traceability table built from the epic summary
  alone would miss them.

  **Checks that came out clean**
  - DESIGN.md's token counts match Story 1.4: 26 colors, 17 typography roles and
  5 radii. Its extra `components` group only refers back to those tokens.
  - The addendum marks its SQLite, Vercel and Neon parts as history.
  - `technology.md` and `teknologinotat.md` add nothing for Epic 1.

  **Not done:** CLAUDE.md asks for a `sesjonslogg` entry at the end of a
  session. That skill creates a log file and commits it, which this task
  forbade, so the parent session should do it.

  I checked the five sources against Story 1.1 part A. Their requirements mostly
  match the story, but none of the exact pins or concrete settings appear in
  them. Those exist only in epics.md (the architecture spine stands behind
  them). Line references use these aliases:
  - PRD = <repo>/_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/prds
  /prd-G40-lyamouri-2026-11-02/prd.md
  - ADD = <repo>/_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/prds
  /prd-G40-lyamouri-2026-11-02/addendum.md

  **1. Requirements**
  - **NFR-1** (PRD:710): no paid services, API keys or third-party accounts.
  Every tool the README asks for is free for personal and educational use. After
  installation everything runs offline, with no CDN. The README covers macOS
  and Windows. Defect: NFR-2 is run into the same line.
  - **NFR-3** (PRD:711): the load command creates the session secret when it is
  missing, in a file `.gitignore` excludes. Setup needs no manual file editing,
  and the repo holds no secrets. Optional configuration goes in `.env.example`.
  The database has no stored password and accepts connections only from the same
  computer.
  - **NFR-4** (PRD:712): no personal data or local file paths in files, and AI
  sessions are cleaned before they are committed.
  - **NFR-6** (PRD:715, 719): no sideways page scrolling from 320 px. The real
  phone reaches the app over the local network, which the app listens on "only
  during the test" (also EXP:283 for the iPhone 13, and AVK:21). There is no
  viewport-tag rule in these sources.
  - **NFR-9** (PRD:722) and **FR-4** (PRD:218): one app and one local
  PostgreSQL, "started with one documented command".
  - **DR-1, setup part** (PRD:739–743):
    - prerequisites with minimum versions;
    - exact commands for macOS Terminal and PowerShell, "run in the project's
  environment";
    - how to start and stop Prognose and its database;
    - how the secret is created;
    - fixes for a database that is not started and a port in use;
    - at the top, how to start, and that v1 is not published, with the reason.
  - **DR-2** (PRD:751–755): the README explains every top-level folder,
  including the BMAD and Claude Code folders. The root holds only the README, a
  license and config files. The `.gitignore` list matches the story. Seed data
  is versioned, the database is not. One formatter and one linter. Dependencies
  are "pinned to exact versions in one file". The root today also holds
  `.impeccable/` and `CLAUDE.md`.
  - **DR-8** (PRD:771): part B, except "The README links it".
  - **DR-9** (PRD:772): each AI defect is logged with how it was found, the fix
  and the commit or PR. Manual checks record the date, OS, browser and result. A
  macOS database test from 2026-11-17 is already logged (PRD:924, ADD:90).
  - **D-3 and Windows on ARM**: neither appears in the five sources.
    - D-3 (EPI:49–54): the Windows x64 and offline tests are Story 1.3, which
  blocks nothing.
    - Windows on ARM is excluded only in EPI:353 ("psycopg has no wheel for it")
  and EPI:387.

  **2. Versions**
  - The five sources contain no exact pins.
    - ADD:55 gives research figures that the addendum says are not decisions
  (ADD:14, 24): Django 6.1.2 was the latest as of 2026-11-01, and 5.2 LTS is
  supported until April 2028.
    - ADD:90 says PostgreSQL 18 runs in Docker Compose.
    - The re-check rule covers the stack's version claims "by 2026-12-08"
  (ADD:55, 170).
  - The exact stack exists only in EPI:359 and matches the story. It adds Docker
  Desktop 4.99.0 with Compose v5.7.0, and uv 0.13.9 when the epics were written
  (0.12.13 tested). Docker Desktop's terms are re-checked before submission
  (EPI:371).

  **3. README wording**
  - No heading text is fixed. PRD:743 says "a section on keys and secrets", and
  AVK:18 says "en egen del om nøkler og hemmeligheter". AVK:14 says the README
  must state how to start the app and which API keys are needed.
  - PRD:743 says the section states: no API key, paid service or third-party
  account is needed; the secret is created automatically and kept out of Git;
  the database has no password; `.env.example` lists only optional variables.
  The file name `.secret_key` is not in these sources.

  **4. UX**
  - **Wordmark:** the text «Prognose».
    - Type: `typography.wordmark`, the system-ui stack at 17 px, weight 700,
  line-height 1.5 (DES:38–42).
    - Colours: white (`on-header`, #FFFFFF) on the navy bar (`header`, #0E2236),
  with 10 × 20 px padding and no logo mark (DES:20–22, 144–148, 426).
    - When logged out, the header shows only the wordmark (EXP:36, 44).
    - No HTML element is set for it, and `h1` is reserved for the page title
  (EXP:265).
  - `lang="nb"` (EXP:262, and NFR-11 at PRD:724), Norwegian bokmål (EXP:20). The
  canvas is #EDF2F7 (DES:343). System fonts only, nothing from a CDN (DES:369,
  477).
  - In the finished app, `/` is Innlogging, "the app root" (EXP:28). Nothing
  describes a placeholder page before login exists.
  - These belong to Story 1.4, not 1.1 (EPI:846–878): the token stylesheet, the
  styled header, the focus ring, the viewport tag `width=device-width,
  initial-scale=1` (EPI:858), the touch rules, the error pages and
  `PROGNOSE_DEBUG`. The login panel is UX-DR8 (EPI:435).

  **5. Conflicts and gaps**
  - The PRD says "macOS and Windows" with no exception (PRD:710, 740, 890). The
  Windows-on-ARM exclusion is not recorded in the PRD.
  - DR-2 asks for pins in "one file", but the story pins in both

  - The story claims DR-1's setup part, but its README criteria leave out the

  `LANGUAGE_CODE`, `TIME_ZONE` and `PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST` appear in none of the

  - `.env` is unsettled. EPI:350 says "environment variables only", and the
  stack has no dotenv package. Yet Story 1.4 turns DEBUG on through "the local
  `.env`" (EPI:877–878). The spec should decide whether the settings read
  `.env`.

✻ Cooked for 49s · done Friday 1:29 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  I only read the archived tag. No files were created or changed, and I ran no
  git command that changes state.

  **1. Verdicts (archived file → new target)**
  - `prognose/env.py` → `src/prognose/env.py`: adapt. Rewrite `database_config`
  and drop the Vercel code.
  - `prognose/settings.py` → `src/prognose/settings.py`: adapt (see 2 and 4).
  - `prognose/tests.py` → `tests/test_settings.py`: adapt. Lines 334-356 (the
  AD-1 test) move to Story 1.2.
  - `prognose/urls.py` and `wsgi.py` → `src/prognose/`: adapt. Drop `helse/` and
  the Vercel docstring.
  - `accounts/models.py` → `src/web/models.py`: reuse as-is, with
  `AUTH_USER_MODEL = "web.Company"`.
  - `accounts/migrations/0001_initial.py`: drop. Generate it again in
  `src/web/migrations/`.
  - `accounts/tests.py` → `tests/test_company.py`: adapt to pytest-django and
  `web.migrations`.
  - `load_seed.py` → `src/web/management/commands/load_seed.py`: reuse as-is.
  Only the docstring changes: the loading now comes in Story 1.7.
  - `planning/tests.py` → `tests/test_load_seed.py` and
  `tests/test_start_page.py`: adapt 22-45 and 90-123; drop 48-87.
  - `planning/views.py` → `src/web/views.py`: keep `start` (10-13); drop
  `health`.
  - `templates/base.html` and `planning/start.html` → `src/web/templates/web/`:
  adapt. Keep `lang="nb"`, the viewport tag and the `data:,` icon. Drop the
  start-page sentence, which is not approved copy.
  - `templates/404.html` and `500.html`: drop; Story 1.4 builds the error pages.
  - `static/css/app.css`: drop. Story 1.4 builds the token stylesheet in
  `src/web/static/web/`.
  - `ruff.toml` → `[tool.ruff]` in `pyproject.toml`: adapt. Keep the excludes,
  including `migrations`, and the `DJ` rules.
  - `requirements.txt`: drop. `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` replace it, and its
  pins are out of date.
  - `.env.example`: adapt. List `PROGNOSE_DEBUG`, `DATABASE_URL` and
  `PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST`, commented out so copying the file to `.env` changes
  nothing. Remove the language-model and Vercel lines.
  - `.gitignore`: `main` already has the archived version unchanged (commit
  d67211c). Remove lines 1-14, `db.sqlite3*` and `staticfiles/`. The story asks
  it to ignore "the local database data", but the named Docker volume leaves no
  database files in the repository.
  - `vercel.json`: drop (AD-16).
  - `README.md`: keep the intro and adapt the notes at 45-49. Replace 13-43 and
  51-59 with the Docker Compose and `uv run` commands, and drop 61-78.
  - `manage.py` (not on your list) → `src/manage.py`: change its error message
  at 13-16, which tells the user to activate a virtual environment and use pip.

  **2. Logic worth carrying over**
  - **Creating the secret file**, `load_seed.py` 17-26: if the file exists, it
  prints a Norwegian notice and returns without reading the file. Otherwise it
  creates the file with `os.open(O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL, 0o600)`, so only the
  owner can read it and an existing file is never overwritten. The content is
  `secrets.token_urlsafe(50)`. The `SECRET_KEY_FILE` setting (settings 27-28)
  lets tests point at a temporary path.
    - Keep this step before Story 1.7's empty-database stop. A Docker volume
  reused from an earlier clone can hold data while `.secret_key` is missing.
  - **When the secret is missing**, `env.py` 115-125: a missing or empty file
  gives a random key per process, kept in memory. Settings never write the file,
  so `migrate` works before `load_seed`.
  - **Reading `.env`**, `env.py` 31-55: the file is optional, read as
  `utf-8-sig`, skips comments, strips one pair of quotes and does not change
  `os.environ`.
    - DEBUG comes only from `.env` (settings 36-37, test 276-280). Story 1.4's
  AC requires this, while AD-13's wording is looser. The variable is renamed
  `PROGNOSE_DEBUG`.
  - **`DATABASE_URL`**, `env.py` 67-78: parsed with `urlsplit`, only `postgres`
  and `postgresql` accepted, parts decoded with `unquote`, and no error repeats
  the URL. An empty value should mean the Compose default.
  - **`ALLOWED_HOSTS`**: settings line 38, plus a new pure helper that adds a
  stripped `PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST` and refuses `*` and `0.0.0.0`.
  - **Tests**:
    - `prognose/tests.py` 39-116, 155-168 and 171-274. The last range starts the
  real settings in a subprocess with a cleaned environment and a stubbed
  `.env`, and checks the secret-file path and console logging. Run it from
  `src/`.
    - `planning/tests.py` 90-123 and `accounts/tests.py` 12-51.
    - The `LOGGING` block in settings 100-108.

  **3. Review findings and lessons that still apply**
  - VG-1/BH-10: tests must stub `.env` and remove `PROGNOSE_*` and
  `DATABASE_URL` from the environment.
  - VG-2: start the real settings with a non-default `DATABASE_URL`, not only
  the helper.
  - VG-3: `assertLogs` replaces the log handlers, so test console logging
  through the subprocess.
  - VG-O1/BH-8/EC-12: an empty `.secret_key` stays empty, because the AC forbids
  overwriting it. Decide whether settings should stop with an error.
  - EC-1/EC-2: an unencoded `/?#`, a bad port, or a missing host or database
  name gives a raw `ValueError` or a late failure.
  - EC-8/EC-9: `.env` files in UTF-16, with `export` or with inline comments are
  not supported.
  - EC-14/15/16: `create_user` neither strips the customer number nor requires a
  name, and `createsuperuser` does not work.
  - BH-7/EC-22: AD-1 now allows only the standard library, so Story 1.2's import
  test should check against `sys.stdlib_module_names`.
  - BH-13 (the favicon link) and the tuple for `REQUIRED_FIELDS` (Ruff rule
  RUF012) still apply.
  - Ruff also checks Python code blocks in Markdown. Limit it to `*.py`, as
  AD-17 says.
  - Process:
    - Test the README on a copy of only the committed files.
    - Prove each test by breaking the code it checks.
    - Count review findings, not patches (corrected in 6849f53).
    - Each test names its FR-n (AD-17).
  - The new spine has already resolved BH-6 (migration check) and BH-9/EC-17
  (Norwegian 400/403 pages). All Vercel findings no longer apply.

  **4. Conflicts that must not be copied**
  - The SQLite fallback: env 65-66, settings 40-44, tests 84-90.
  - The Neon connection options: env 79-81, tests 102-111. The official
  `postgres` image has no SSL, so `sslmode=require` would refuse to connect.
  - The Vercel run mode: env 18-28 and 85-112, settings 25-33, tests 119-152 and
  290-331. Its secure cookies would break login over plain HTTP in the phone
  test.
  - `BASE_DIR` (settings 23) would point at `src/`, but `.secret_key` and `.env`
  belong at the repository root. The `.gitignore` rule for `.secret_key`
  matches at any depth, so a misplaced file would go unnoticed.
  - `/helse/`: a route open without login that is neither in AD-11's URL map nor
  among AD-6's exemptions.
  - The app names `accounts` and `planning`, and the `templates/` and `static/`
  folders at the root.
  - The hard-coded CSS colours and fonts, which AD-11 forbids.
  - The error-page text «Ikke funnet»/«Til startsiden». UX-DR13 requires «Siden
  finnes ikke»/«Til produktoversikten».
  - Also do not copy:
    - the virtual-environment and pip instructions, and `manage.py test`;
    - the variable name `DEBUG` and the language-model variables;
    - the old AD numbers in docstrings;
    - the old spec's rule "no `pyproject.toml`".

  hvordan hemmelighetsfila lages uten å overskrive, hvordan .env leses og

  feller jeg unngår, blant annet Vercel, Neon, SQLite og en BASE_DIR som pekte
  på src/. Nå skriver jeg spesifikasjonen.

  Part A of Story 1.1 is implemented as the spec describes, and every
  verification step passed: 69 tests, Ruff lint and format, and the clean-copy
  run. Everything is uncommitted. I did not edit the spec (its task boxes are
  still unticked for your verification step) or `sprint-status.yaml`, and I did
  not run `sesjonslogg`.

  ## What changed (paths under <repo>)
  - **New config:** `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `.python-version` and
  `compose.yaml` (the `db` service as AD-14 describes).
    - `pyproject.toml` pins Django 5.2.19 and psycopg[binary] 3.3.9, with pytest
  9.2.0, pytest-django 4.14.0, ruff 0.17.5 and openpyxl 3.1.5 in the `dev`
  group. It sets `package = false`, the pytest settings, and Ruff with `include
  = ["*.py"]`, the three excludes and the extra rules B, DJ, I and UP.
    - `uv.lock` has psycopg wheels for macOS arm64 and x86_64 and for Windows
  x64, and none for Windows on ARM.
    - The health check in `compose.yaml` uses `pg_isready` over TCP, so it
  cannot report healthy while the image's first-start server is still running.
  - **New code:** `src/manage.py` (with a uv hint),
  `src/prognose/{__init__,env,settings,urls,wsgi}.py`,
  `src/forecasting/__init__.py`, `src/explanation/__init__.py`,
  `src/web/{__init__,apps,models,views}.py`,
  `src/web/migrations/0001_initial.py` (generated),
  `src/web/management/commands/load_seed.py`,
  `src/web/templates/web/start.html`, `data/.gitkeep` and `reference/.gitkeep`.
    - `staticfiles` is installed so that `runserver --insecure` works.
    - Nothing from Stories 1.2–1.8 is included: no CI, no
  `LoginRequiredMiddleware`, no `LOGGING`, no error pages, no base template and
  no stylesheet.
  - **New tests:** `tests/test_settings.py`, `tests/test_company.py`,
  `tests/test_load_seed.py` and `tests/test_start_page.py`. Each test's
  docstring names its IDs.
  - **Edited:** `.gitignore` (the Next.js block, `db.sqlite3*` and
  `staticfiles/` removed), `README.md` («Kom i gang», «Nøkler og hemmeligheter»,
  «Tester»; intro and member list kept) and `docs/qa-logg.md` (a new 2026-11-20
  entry).

  ## How it was verified
  - **Locked install:** `uv sync --locked` left `uv.lock` unchanged.
  - **Database:** `docker compose up -d --wait` reported healthy, published on
  127.0.0.1:15432 only.
  - **Migrate and `load_seed`:** `migrate` applied 16 migrations, including
  `web.0001_initial`. `load_seed` created `.secret_key` with mode 600, and the
  second run left it byte-identical (same sha256). Git ignores the file.
  - **Tests and Ruff:**
    - `uv run pytest`: 69 passed, none skipped.
    - `uv run ruff check`: passed.
    - `uv run ruff format --check`: 19 files already formatted.
    - `ruff check --show-files` lists only files in `src/` and `tests/`, with
  migrations excluded.
  - **Mutation check:** I added a model field without a migration, and the
  `makemigrations --check` test failed as it should. I reverted the field.
  - **Errors through `manage.py`:** a bad `DATABASE_URL` or `PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST`
  stops with a Norwegian `ImproperlyConfigured` that does not show the password.
  - **Clean-copy run:** a scratchpad copy of the tracked and new files, with its
  own Compose project and volume, both removed afterwards. The repo's database
  was stopped during the run because both use port 15432.

    |---|---|
    | `docker compose up -d --wait` | 3.1 s |
    | `uv sync --locked` | 0.2 s |

  127.0.0.1:8000 only and the database on 127.0.0.1:15432 only. The `prognose`

  - **Blank secret:** `test_blank_secret_stops_and_stays_unchanged` (empty, and
  whitespace only)

  ## Choices the spec leaves open (worth a review look)
  - **DEBUG:** `PROGNOSE_DEBUG` in the environment can turn DEBUG off but never
  on. I read "the environment wins" as applying here too; the spec's "only when
  `.env` sets it" still holds.
  - **`.env` comments:** a `#` after whitespace ends an unquoted value. A line
  without `=` is skipped silently, and `export KEY=` is not supported.
  - **`PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST`:** only private IPv4 addresses (10.x, 172.16–31.x,
  192.168.x) are accepted. IPv6, loopback, link-local and 100.64/10 are refused,
  and the error message does not repeat the value.
  - **`DATABASE_URL`:** port 0 is refused, a missing port means 5432, and query
  parameters are ignored.
  - **Damaged `.secret_key`:** a file that is not UTF-8 raises a Norwegian
  `ImproperlyConfigured`. The matrix doesn't cover this case.
  - **`REQUIRED_FIELDS`:** it is a tuple, because Ruff 0.17.5's default rules
  flagged the list (RUF012). That is a defect in AI-written code, fixed before
  any commit, which you may want to record under DR-9.
  - **`/` with POST:** `/` accepts only GET and HEAD, so a POST gets Django's
  blank 405 response until Story 1.4 adds error pages. Until then, with DEBUG
  off and no `LOGGING`, server errors are not printed to the console either.
  - **README minimum versions:** I used the tested versions, uv 0.12.13 and
  Docker Desktop 4.99.0. The «macOS 14 or newer» line comes from the 2026-11-17
  QA entry.

  ## Local state and work outside the project
  - **Still running:** the repo's database container `g40-lyamouri-db-1` (volume
  `g40-lyamouri_pgdata`). Stop it with `docker compose stop`. `.venv/` and
  `.secret_key` exist in the repo; both are ignored by Git.
  - **Scratchpad:** the clean-copy script
  `<tmp>/adf59768-389e-4c07-
  adbd-26798701f1f5/scratchpad/clean_copy_run.sh` and its logs in
  `<tmp>/adf59768-389e-4c07-
  adbd-26798701f1f5/scratchpad/logs/`. A `pin-check` folder there is not mine; I
  left it alone.
  - **Outside the project folder** (the user's memory note asks to stay inside
  it): besides the scratchpad, I read Docker Desktop's Info.plist to get its
  version for the QA log, and used the `$HOME/.docker/bin` path the spec names.
  I also wrote a backup of `models.py` to the system temp folder by mistake and
  deleted it at once.

      77  ## Implementation Notes
      78  
      79 +- Decisions within the spec: the environment can turn DEBUG off
         + but never on; a `#` after whitespace ends an unquoted `.env` v
         +alue, lines without `=` are skipped and `export` is not support
         +ed; `PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST` accepts only RFC 1918 IPv4 addresses; a
         + `DATABASE_URL` without a port uses 5432, port 0 is refused and
         + query parameters are ignored; a `.secret_key` that is not UTF-
         +8 raises `ImproperlyConfigured`.                               
      80 +- The health check runs `pg_isready` over TCP, because the imag
         +e's first-start server listens only on its socket and would rep
         +ort healthy too early.                                         
      81 +- `/` shows «Prognose» as its `h1` with the approved line from 
         +the Innlogging wireframe; the navy header with the wordmark is 
         +Story 1.4, and the login page at `/` is Story 1.8. A POST to `/
         +` gets Django's blank 405 until Story 1.4 adds the error pages.
      82 +- `REQUIRED_FIELDS` is a tuple, because Ruff flagged the list (
         +RUF012) during implementation, before any commit.              
      83 +- Verification: 69 tests passed with none skipped, Ruff passed 
         +on the 19 Python files in `src/` and `tests/`, and the clean-co
         +py run passed twice (7.8 s and 7.2 s in total, image and packag
         +es cached), with the app on `127.0.0.1:8000` only, the database
         + on `127.0.0.1:15432` only and no password on the `prognose` ro
         +le.

✻ Brewed for 7m 23s · done Friday 5:49 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  N = min(floor(sqrt(77.7) + 1), 10) = min(floor(9.81), 10) = 9 (the diff is
  77,683 bytes, about 77.7 kB).

  - **`.gitignore` has no regression guard.** The rules are correct today: I
  checked with `git check-ignore`, which shows `.env` and `.secret_key` ignored
  and `.env.example` kept. But keeping the secret out of Git rests on one line
  at `<repo>/.gitignore:56`, and Story 1.1's
  acceptance criterion ("creates `.secret_key`, which `.gitignore` excludes") is
  only checked by hand. A small test (`git check-ignore -q .secret_key .env`,
  and `.env.example` not ignored) would catch a later edit that drops that line.

  - **The README states Windows support that has not been tested.**
  `<repo>/README.md:9` and `:17` say the commands
  work in PowerShell and that Windows 10/11 on x64 and Intel Macs are supported.
  The QA log says the Windows setup belongs to Story 1.3, and only an M1 Mac
  was used. DR-1 asks for a setup "tested by the group", so mark Windows and
  Intel macOS as not yet tested until Story 1.3 confirms them.

  - **The README doesn't say which commands are one-time.**
  `<repo>/README.md:28` says to run all five
  commands, with nothing on which to repeat on the next start (`docker compose
  up -d --wait` and `runserver --insecure`, plus `migrate` after pulling).
  Re-running is harmless today. But under AD-15, Story 1.7's `load_seed` "stops
  with a Norwegian message" once a company exists, so anyone who follows the
  README a second time will fail at step 4. Stop commands are deferred to Story
  4.1, but starting again is part of DR-1's "how to start".

  - **README «Tester» is vague about which database the tests use.**
  `<repo>/README.md:55` says the tests use the local
  database, which suggests they touch the app's data. In fact pytest-django
  creates and drops a separate `test_prognose` database on the same server. That
  needs the right to create databases: the Compose superuser has it, but a role
  behind a custom `DATABASE_URL` may not. Saying both would reassure the
  examiner and explain a possible failure.

  - **Ruff's scope is described more narrowly than it is configured.**
  `<repo>/README.md:64` and the comment at
  `<repo>/pyproject.toml:30` say Ruff checks only the
  Python files in `src/` and `tests/`, citing AD-17. The configuration is
  actually a blocklist: `include = ["*.py"]` minus `_bmad`, `.claude` and
  `**/migrations`.
    - `src/web/migrations/` is inside `src/` but is not checked.
    - Any new Python file elsewhere joins silently, for example AD-15's
  `data/generate_seed.py` in Story 1.6.
    - AD-17 only says "Python files only", not "only `src/` and `tests/`".

    Reword both to match the configuration, or switch to an explicit allowlist.

  - **The QA-log entry leaves out part of the verification.** In
  `<repo>/docs/qa-logg.md:94-116`:
    - The spec says the clean-copy run passed twice (7.8 s and 7.2 s), but only
  one run is recorded.
    - The per-command times add up to 7.5 s against the 7.8 s total, and the gap
  is not explained.
    - The run used a copy of "the files to be committed", not the `git clone`
  the acceptance criterion names. So `git clone`, `cd` and the real committed
  set of files were never exercised.

    A «Gjenstår» item to repeat the five commands from a clone of the pushed
  branch before merging is missing.

  - **`.env.example` doesn't say that `DATABASE_URL` credentials must be
  percent-encoded.** Neither `<repo>/.env.example:13`
  nor the error message in
  `<repo>/src/prognose/env.py` mentions it.
    - I ran it: a password containing `#` (`postgresql://u:pa#ss@h:5433/x`)
  gives the misleading "DATABASE_URL har en ugyldig vert eller port".
    - Query parameters such as `?sslmode=require` are dropped without any
  message (`env.py:121`). That is a spec decision, but it deserves either a
  refusal or at least a note.

  - **The phone-test command listens on every network interface.** The command
  at `<repo>/.env.example:19` (`runserver --insecure
  0.0.0.0:8000`, taken from AD-13) does this. `ALLOWED_HOSTS` only checks the
  Host header, which any client can set, so it is not access control. Starting
  with `runserver --insecure <PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST>:8000` limits the app to the one
  private network being tested, which fits NFR-3. This needs a small change to
  AD-13.

  - **`pyproject.toml` doesn't enforce the tool versions the README and Stack
  promise.**
    - The README requires uv 0.12.13 or newer, but there is no `[tool.uv]
  required-version = ">=0.12.13"`
  (`<repo>/pyproject.toml:19`). An older uv is not
  stopped with a clear version message.
    - `requires-python = ">=3.13"` (`pyproject.toml:5`) is wider than the
  Stack's "Python 3.13" and `.python-version`. That is why `uv.lock` carries
  cp314 and cp315 wheels, which allows untested interpreters. `"==3.13.*"` would
  match.

  - **The `.env` parser reads a comment after an empty value as the value.**
  Confirmed by running it: `PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST=   # fylles ut ved telefontesten`
  gives the value `# fylles ut ved telefontesten`, so the settings refuse to
  load with the LAN-host error. The cause is in
  `<repo>/src/prognose/env.py:59`: the value is
  stripped before the inline-comment pattern (`\s+#`) runs, so a value that
  starts with `#` never counts as a comment. Treat a leading `#` as an empty
  value, and add the case to the `.env` test.

  - **The `.env` parser silently ignores unsupported or misspelled lines.** In
  `read_dotenv` (`<repo>/src/prognose/env.py:34`),
  `export PROGNOSE_DEBUG=1` is stored under the key `"export PROGNOSE_DEBUG"`,
  and a typo like `PROGNOSE_DEGUB=1` is also just stored. I confirmed both by
  running them: DEBUG stays off with no message. The spec says `export` is not
  supported, but nothing tells the user and no test pins the behaviour. Either
  strip `export ` or refuse it, and warn about unknown `PROGNOSE_*` keys.

  - **Only two file errors get the required Norwegian message.** `read_dotenv`
  and `read_secret_key`
  (`<repo>/src/prognose/env.py:43` and `:163`)
  translate only `FileNotFoundError` and `UnicodeDecodeError`.
    - I confirmed by running it that a `.env` or `.secret_key` that is a
  directory gives a raw `IsADirectoryError` while the settings load, which shows
  an English traceback.
    - That happens, for example, with a virtual environment made by `python -m
  venv .env`, a case the `.gitignore` itself anticipates. A `PermissionError`
  behaves the same way.

    This breaks the spec rule that configuration errors raise
  `ImproperlyConfigured` in Norwegian. Catch `OSError` and name the fix in the
  message.

  - **A missing `.secret_key` falls back to a random key with no warning.** The
  fallback in `load_secret_key`
  (`<repo>/src/prognose/env.py:179`) is intentional,
  so that `migrate` and CI can run before `load_seed`. The side effect is that a
  skipped `load_seed` or a deleted `.secret_key` goes unnoticed, and every
  process, including each `runserver` autoreload, gets a new key. Once Story 1.8
  adds login, every code change will log the user out and break open forms'
  CSRF tokens, with no hint why. A Norwegian warning from Django's system checks
  ("kjør load_seed") when the file is missing, outside tests, would make this
  visible.

  - **`create_user` doesn't enforce the company name.** `REQUIRED_FIELDS =
  ("name",)` at `<repo>/src/web/models.py:30` signals
  that the name is required. But `create_user` (`models.py:8`) only checks the
  customer number, so `Company.objects.create_user("100002", "pw")` saves a
  company with an empty name. No test covers this.

  - **The BOM test cannot detect a BOM regression.** Confirmed by running it: in
  `<repo>/tests/test_settings.py:227-241`, if
  `read_dotenv` reads with plain `utf-8` instead of `utf-8-sig`, the result is
  identical and the test still passes. The BOM sits in front of `# Lokale
  innstillinger`, a comment line with no `=`, which is skipped whether or not
  the BOM is removed. The BOM is also an invisible U+FEFF character inside `""`
  at line 241, which an editor can strip without anyone noticing. Put a
  `KEY=VALUE` line first and write the BOM as `""`.

  - **The 405 test cites decisions that contradict it.**
  `<repo>/tests/test_start_page.py:38` justifies "a
  POST is refused with 405" with AD-10 and AD-11. But AD-11's URL map makes `/`
  the login page (Innlogging) with GET and POST, and AD-12 requires a Norwegian
  405 page rather than Django's blank one. Mark the test as a placeholder until
  Stories 1.4 and 1.8 replace it, so its DR-5 traceability doesn't point the
  wrong way.

  - **The external-resource check is easy to bypass.** The pattern at
  `<repo>/tests/test_start_page.py:34` only matches
  double-quoted, lowercase `src="…//` or `href="…//`. It misses:
    - single quotes, unquoted attributes and uppercase schemes;
    - `srcset` and `action`;
    - CSS `url()` or `@import` in `<style>` or `style=`.

    Story 1.4's base template will rely on this test as its NFR-1 guard. Parse
  the HTML with `html.parser` and check every attribute that holds a URL, or
  narrow the test's name and docstring.

  Verification Gap før jeg vurderer noe, slik BMAD krever.

› Message from general-purpose
  I found three verification gaps in the Story 1.1 diff, and no other defects.
  Each one is backed by a mutation run on scratch copies that left the existing
  tests green. The repo was only read: `git status` is unchanged, and no
  database tests ran, so the local database was not touched. There is no CI yet
  (`.github/` is absent; CI is Story 1.2), so `uv run pytest` is the only
  automated check. Everything else in the diff is either verified by a test that
  would fail on a realistic regression, or is static text (README, QA log,
  `.env.example`, `.gitignore`, `compose.yaml`).

  ### The refused-DATABASE_URL test cannot see a password leaked through the
  chained `ValueError`, so `from None` is unprotected

  - **Changed surface:** `database_config` hides urllib's `ValueError` with
  `raise _database_url_error("har en ugyldig vert eller port") from None` —
  `src/prognose/env.py:127-131`. It serves the spec rule that configuration
  errors "never echo the URL or a secret" (`_bmad-output/implementation-artifact
  s/spec-1-1-start-prognose-locally-from-a-fresh-clone.md:28`).
  - **Impacted consumer or site:** `DATABASES = {"default":
  env.database_config(...)}` at `src/prognose/settings.py:33`. It runs on every
  settings import. I ran `DATABASE_URL='postgresql://u:hemmelig/x' python
  src/manage.py check`: it exits 1 and prints a full Python traceback. This is
  the same path as the three `manage.py` commands at `README.md:33-35`.
  - **Existing test evidence:** `Broken-verification gap`.
    - `test_unusable_database_url_is_refused_without_repeating_it`
  (`tests/test_settings.py:282-302`) asserts `"hemmelig" not in
  shown_error(caught.value)` at line 302. `shown_error` (lines 67-69) includes
  chained exceptions, so the test is meant to catch exactly this.
    - In all 8 URLs the password sits before `@`, where urllib never quotes it.
  The chained `ValueError` texts are only "Port could not be cast to integer
  value as 'port'", "Port out of range 0-65535" and "Invalid IPv6 URL".
    - By contrast, the secret-file test (lines 328-337) does observe its own
  `from None` through "0xff".
  - **Missing verification:** a refused URL where urllib's error text contains
  the password, checked against the shown traceback.
  - **Demonstration:** I made two mutants of a scratch copy of env.py: one with
  `from None` removed, one with `except ValueError as error: ... from error`.
  Ruff's enabled bugbear rule B904 accepts both forms. Both mutants pass all 8
  cases with the test's own assertions. For the URLs below, both mutants print
  `ValueError: Port could not be cast to integer value as 'hemmelig'`; the
  current code prints nothing of it:
    - `postgresql://prognose:hemmelig/2026@127.0.0.1:15432/prognose` (unencoded
  `/` in the password)
    - `...:hemmelig?x@...` (unencoded `?`)
    - `postgresql://u:hemmelig/x` (host forgotten)
  - **Consequence:** a refactor that drops or changes `from None` passes the
  suite, and every mistyped DATABASE_URL then prints all or part of the database
  password in the terminal (NFR-3).
  - **Disposition:** `patch` — add `("postgresql://u:hemmelig/x", "ugyldig vert
  eller port")` to the parametrize list at `tests/test_settings.py:282-293`. I
  checked that it passes on the current code and fails on both mutants.

  ### Percent-decoding of the DATABASE_URL user and database name is unpinned,
  though the test docstring claims it

  - **Changed surface:** `unquote` on NAME and USER — `src/prognose/env.py:138`
  and `:144`. The docstring at `src/prognose/env.py:124` says "The user, the
  password and the database name may be percent-encoded."
  - **Impacted consumer or site:** `settings.DATABASES["default"]` built at
  `src/prognose/settings.py:33` from the optional DATABASE_URL. Django's
  PostgreSQL backend uses its USER and NAME to connect.
  - **Existing test evidence:** `Broken-verification gap`.
    - `test_custom_database_url` (`tests/test_settings.py:267-272`) says "with
  the user, password and name percent-encoded" (lines 268-269).
    - But `CUSTOM_URL = "postgres://u:p%40ss@h:5433/x"` (line 23) encodes only
  the password; `u` and `x` decode to themselves.
    - The other success-path inputs (lines 72-94, 190-213, 275-279) contain no
  encoded characters either.
  - **Missing verification:** an assertion that an encoded user and name are
  decoded.
  - **Demonstration:** I removed `unquote` from USER and NAME in a scratch copy.
  Every success-path assertion in the settings tests and all 8 refused-URL
  cases still pass. For `postgresql://min%40bruker@h:5433/min%2Ddb` the mutant
  returns USER `'min%40bruker'` and NAME `'min%2Ddb'`; the current code returns
  `min@bruker` and `min-db`.
  - **Consequence:** a developer whose database user or name contains a reserved
  character gets an authentication or "database does not exist" error, and no
  test fails.
  - **Disposition:** `patch` — add a separate test, for example
  `test_database_url_decodes_the_user_and_name`, and keep the frozen spec matrix
  row and `CUSTOM_URL` as they are. I checked that the current code returns
  `min@bruker` and `min db` for
  `postgresql://min%40bruker:p%40ss@h:5433/min%20db`. If encoded user and name
  are not meant to be supported, narrow both docstrings to the password instead.

  ### README step 5 (`runserver --insecure`) is never exercised: dropping
  `staticfiles` or breaking `WSGI_APPLICATION` keeps `uv run pytest` green

  - **Changed surface:**
    - `"django.contrib.staticfiles"` in `INSTALLED_APPS`, with the comment "Also
  gives runserver its --insecure option" — `src/prognose/settings.py:43-44`.
  Spec line 61 says "`staticfiles` installed so `--insecure` works".
    - `WSGI_APPLICATION = "prognose.wsgi.application"` —
  `src/prognose/settings.py:58`, pointing at `src/prognose/wsgi.py:9`.
  - **Impacted consumer or site:** `uv run python src/manage.py runserver
  --insecure` at `README.md:35` (explained at `:42`). This is the command in
  spec acceptance criterion 1 (spec line 71).
  - **Existing test evidence:** `Regression gap`.
    - I grepped tests/ and src/ for `runserver`, `insecure`, `staticfiles`,
  `wsgi`, `WSGI_APPLICATION`, `get_internal_wsgi_application`, `manage.py`,
  `get_commands`, `load_command_class` and `subprocess`. Nothing in tests/
  matches; the only hits are the settings comment, wsgi.py and docstrings in
  src/.
    - The only `call_command` uses are `makemigrations`
  (`tests/test_company.py:44`) and `load_seed` (`tests/test_load_seed.py:26`).
    - `tests/test_start_page.py` uses Django's test client, which loads neither
  the runserver command nor `WSGI_APPLICATION`.
    - The only coverage is the agent-run clean-copy setup in `docs/qa-logg.md`,
  which `uv run pytest` does not repeat.
  - **Missing verification:** a check that the active `runserver` comes from
  `django.contrib.staticfiles` and accepts `--insecure`, and that
  `get_internal_wsgi_application()` loads.
  - **Demonstration:**
    - With a scratch settings module that drops staticfiles, all 66 non-database
  tests pass, and the real `src/manage.py runserver --insecure` stops with
  `manage.py runserver: error: unrecognized arguments: --insecure`.
    - With `WSGI_APPLICATION = "prognose.wsgi.app"`, the same 66 tests pass, but
  `get_internal_wsgi_application()` raises "WSGI application
  'prognose.wsgi.app' could not be loaded". runserver loads the app through this
  setting at startup.
    - I didn't run the 3 database tests, but I read them: makemigrations check,
  PBKDF2 hash and load_seed file creation. None of them touches static files,
  runserver or WSGI.
  - **Consequence:** the story's main acceptance criterion, starting Prognose
  with the README's fifth command, can break while `uv run pytest` stays green.
  The same holds for the CI planned in Story 1.2, which will run that suite.
  - **Disposition:** `patch` — add a test that needs no database, for example
  `tests/test_start_command.py`, with a docstring naming AD-13 and NFR-1 (DR-5).
  It should assert:
    - `get_commands()["runserver"] == "django.contrib.staticfiles"`;
    - `load_command_class("django.contrib.staticfiles",
  "runserver").create_parser("manage.py",
  "runserver").parse_args(["--insecure"]).insecure_serving` is True;
    - `get_internal_wsgi_application()` returns a handler.

    Optionally, `subprocess.run([sys.executable, "src/manage.py", "check"])`
  would also cover manage.py's settings default, which no test runs today.

  The mutation scripts are in `<tmp>/-Users-<bruker>-G40-
  lyamouri/adf59768-389e-4c07-adbd-26798701f1f5/scratchpad/vg-review/`
  (`check_env_mutations.py`, `check_start_path.py`, `settings_mut/`).

✻ Brewed for 5m 35s · done Friday 6:17 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  I've fixed all 16 points. The affected test files (`test_settings.py`,
  `test_load_seed.py`, `test_start_page.py` and the new `test_start_command.py`)
  pass: 70 tests, with `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=annet.settings` exported, and
  pytest-django reported `settings: prognose.settings (from option)`. I didn't
  run `test_company.py`, since nothing it covers changed. Ruff check and format
  pass on the edited Python files, and nothing is committed.

  **`src/prognose/env.py`**
  - **Comment-only value:** an unquoted value that starts with `#` is now empty.
  I did this by changing the comment pattern to `(?:^|\s+)#.*$`, so
  `PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST=   # fylles ut` gives `""`.
  - **`export` prefix:** a leading `export ` is stripped from the key.
  - **Unreadable files:** `read_dotenv` and `read_secret_key` now catch
  `OSError` after the existing handlers. They raise a Norwegian
  `ImproperlyConfigured` that names the file and the fix, using `from None` so
  the original error and its full path are not shown.
  - **Percent-encoding hint:** the shared `DATABASE_URL` error now says to
  percent-encode special characters, for example @ as %40 and # as %23. It still
  doesn't repeat the URL.

  **`tests/test_settings.py`**
  - **BOM test:** the BOM is now the escape `""`. It was an invisible literal
  because the Write tool turned my escape into the character itself.
  `DATABASE_URL=...` now comes first, right after the BOM.
  - **`.env` parsing test:** added `export PROGNOSE_DEBUG=1  # ...`,
  `PROGNOSE_LAN_HOST=   # fylles ut` and a single-quoted line.
  - **Folder tests:** new tests treat `.env` and `.secret_key` as folders. Each
  also checks that the temp path doesn't appear in the error shown.
  - **New URL case:** added `("postgresql://u:hemmelig/x", "ugyldig vert eller
  port")`. I checked that it fails when `from None` is removed, then restored
  it.
  - **Decoding test:** new test that `min%40bruker` and `min%20db` decode to
  `min@bruker` and `min db`. I also narrowed the docstring of
  `test_custom_database_url` to its encoded password.

  **Other code**
  - **`tests/test_start_page.py`:** the POST test now cites only AD-10: the
  start page has no form, so a POST gets 405. The second test is renamed
  `test_start_page_has_a_data_icon_and_no_src_or_href_with_a_host`, with a
  docstring that describes exactly what the regex checks.
  - **`tests/test_start_command.py` (new, no database access, AD-13 and
  NFR-1):** one test checks that `runserver` comes from
  `django.contrib.staticfiles` and accepts `--insecure`. The other checks that
  `get_internal_wsgi_application()` loads a `WSGIHandler`.
  - **`src/manage.py`:** sets `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` unconditionally.
  `manage.py check` passes with the foreign variable exported.
  - **`pyproject.toml`:**
    - `requires-python = "==3.13.*"`.
    - `required-version = ">=0.12.13"` under `[tool.uv]`.
    - `addopts = "--ds=prognose.settings"` replaces the ini's
  `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` line.
    - The Ruff comment now says it checks every `*.py` file except BMAD's and
  Claude Code's scripts and the migrations.
  - **`uv.lock`:** I re-ran `uv lock`. The lock now has `requires-python =
  "==3.13.*"` and only cp313 wheels, and `uv lock --check` passes.

  **Documents**
  - **`.env.example`:** the `DATABASE_URL` comment now says that special
  characters must be percent-encoded and that parameters after `?` are not used.
  - **`README.md`:**
    - It now says the setup so far is tested only on macOS with Apple M1, with a
  link to the QA log.
    - «Tester» explains that pytest creates and deletes its own `test_prognose`
  database on the same server, so the app's data isn't touched. It adds that
  this needs the right to create databases, which the Compose user `prognose`
  has.
    - The Ruff bullet now describes the real scope.
  - **`docs/qa-logg.md`:**
    - The entry now covers both runs, with your timings from run 2 in a second
  time column.
    - One sentence explains that the totals exceed the step sums (7.5 and 7.0 s)
  because they include the time between the commands.
    - A new «Gjenstår» item says to rerun the five commands from a `git clone`
  of the pushed branch before the pull request is merged.

  I wrote run 2's method as "the same checks", because I only have your summary
  of it.

⏺ /export kan jeg ikke kjøre selv, fordi det er en kommando i Claude Code. Skriv
  dette i ledeteksten:

  /export .logs/mounia-03/sessions/1-1-2026-11-21.txt

  Si fra når fila er laget. Hvis Claude Code legger et klokkeslett til i navnet,
  slik det gjorde 20. november, gir jeg fila riktig navn uten å endre
  innholdet.

  Eksporten vil trolig inneholde hele samtalen, også delen fra 20. november som
  allerede ligger i 1-1-2026-11-20.txt. Det sjekker jeg når fila finnes. Før den
  samlede commiten foreslår jeg hvordan vi unngår at den delen står to ganger.

  Etter eksporten tar jeg resten uten subagenter, i denne rekkefølgen:
  1. Jeg maskerer personopplysninger og lokale stier i den nye fila.
  2. Jeg oppdaterer dagens seksjon i sesjonsloggen med del B, klonetesten og
     eksporten, og lukker eksportpunktet i issues.md.
  3. Jeg sjekker at ingenting står dobbelt i sesjonsloggen, issues.md, QA-loggen
     og øktfilene.
  4. Jeg viser deg resultatet og venter på godkjenning før én samlet commit og
     push.

❯ Sjekk om bmad code review er obligatorisk for story 1.1 og hva tidligere      
  gjennomganger allerede dekker. Foreslå den mest effektive måten å oppfylle    
  kravet på uten dobbeltarbeid. Ikke start review, bruk subagenter eller filer  
  ennå.                                                                         

⏺ Jeg sjekker tre kilder og endrer ingenting. Jeg starter heller ikke
  bmad-code-review, men leser skill-filene for å se hvordan den arbeider:
  - kravet i epics.md og PRD-en;
  - hvordan bmad-code-review arbeider;
  - hva reviewene i bmad-build allerede har dekket.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Kravet er tydelig. Nå leser jeg resten av DR-7 og stegene i bmad-code-review:
  hvilken diff den gjennomgår, hvilke granskere den bruker og om den gjenbruker
  funn som allerede er vurdert.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ bmad-code-review tar en spesifikasjonsfil og bruker baseline_commit som
  utgangspunkt for diffen, og jeg sjekker nå hvilke granskere den bruker og om
  den gjenbruker tidligere funn som bmad-build gjør.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Ja, bmad-code-review er obligatorisk for story 1.1. Reviewene i bmad-build
  dekker allerede mye av innholdet, men ikke selve review-sporet som rutinen
  krever.

  Kravet
  - Reglene for alle stories i epics.md sier at pull requesten for hver story
    skal være gjennomgått med BMAD Code Review etter rutinen for code review. Du
    vedtok rutinen 18. november.
  - Rutinen sier at du kjører bmad-code-review på grenen med spesifikasjonen før
    flettingen. Funnene med din beslutning (rettet, avvist eller utsatt) skal
    stå i en kommentar i pull requesten. Hver retting er en egen commit som
    kommentaren nevner.
  - PRD-en krever det samme i DR-7 og SM-8: en pull request som er gjennomgått,
    med kommentarene i pull requesten.

  Det reviewene i bmad-build allerede dekker
  - Samme granskere: begge delene er gjennomgått med tre av de fire granskerne
    bmad-code-review bruker: Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter og Verification Gap.
  - Del A, kode, tester og oppsett: 35 funn, der 20 er rettet, 2 utsatt og 13
    avvist.
  - Del B, dokumentene: 20 funn, og alle er rettet.
  - Hvor de står: vurderingene står i spesifikasjonenes «Review Triage Log», og
    feilene i QA-loggen.
  - Koden etterpå: den er ikke endret siden 93c9147. Senere commits gjelder bare
    dokumenter og logger.

  Det som ikke er dekket
  - Acceptance Auditor: den fjerde granskeren kontrollerer koden mot
    akseptkriteriene i spesifikasjonen, og den finnes bare i bmad-code-review.
  - Formkravene i rutinen: dine beslutninger per funn, kommentaren i pull
    requesten og rettinger som egne commits. Rettingene fra bmad-build ble lagt
    inn før første commit, og det var jeg som vurderte funnene.
  - Gjenbruk: bmad-code-review gjenbruker ikke vurderingene fra bmad-build. En
    vanlig kjøring vil derfor finne mange av de 55 funnene på nytt.

  Mitt forslag, uten dobbeltarbeid
  1. Én kjøring for hele storyen. Kjør bmad-code-review med spesifikasjonen for
     del A. Den har ae7b72c som utgangspunkt og dekker dermed hele grenen.
     Diffen avgrenses til kode, konfigurasjon, tester, README, .env.example,
     compose.yaml og de tre prosessdokumentene. Øktfila på 5 000 linjer,
     sesjonsloggene og BMAD-filene holdes utenfor.
  2. Gjenbruk av vurderte funn. Funn som allerede står i en av de to loggene med
     samme sted og samme påstand, beholder vurderingen sin og skal ikke
     vurderes eller rettes på nytt. Bare nye funn trenger din beslutning. Jeg
     foreslår en beslutning per funn, så du kan godkjenne alle samlet.
  3. Kommentaren i pull requesten. Den lister funnene fra denne kjøringen med
     beslutning og commit, og viser med én linje til de 55 funnene fra
     bmad-build, i stedet for å kopiere dem.
  4. QA-loggen. Bare nye feil i KI-generert kode føres der.

  Rekkefølge
  1. Lag pull requesten. Beskrivelsen må:
     - nevne storyen og si at den ikke dekker noen FR, bare NFR og DR;
     - si hva som er testet, det vil si 75 tester, Ruff, kopi- og klonetest og
       de tre Safari-størrelsene.
  2. Kjør reviewen.
  3. Legg inn rettingene som egne commits.
  4. Skriv kommentaren i pull requesten.

  Reviewen bruker fire subagenter, så jeg trenger din godkjenning for dem når
  den skal kjøres.

  Ett hinder for flettingen: rutinen krever at CI passerer, men CI kommer først
  i story 1.2. For story 1.1 kan pull requesten si at testene og Ruff passerer
  lokalt, og at CI kommer i 1.2. Alternativt venter flettingen til 1.2 er på
  plass. Det må du avgjøre.

  - Tre steder krever CI før fletting:

      locally and in CI»;

    og at pull requesten skal være gjennomgått. Et unntak er altså innenfor
    PRD-en.

    og kontrollen av Implementation Readiness fanget den ikke opp.

     arkitekturspinen, som begge er final.

     uv run ruff check og uv run ruff format --check passerer lokalt på siste
     commit. Den første CI-kjøringen kommer med pull requesten for story 1.2, og
     den kjører alle testene på Linux, også testene fra 1.1. Regelen på linje
     589 og punkt 5 på linje 611 får en henvisning til D-9, og endringsloggen i
     epics.md får en rad.

     endringsloggen, så den ikke motsier epics.md.

     rettingen D-9 og commiten (DR-9).

     på siste commit med commit-hash. Testene kjøres på nytt rett før
     flettingen.

  Punkt 2–4 kan komme i én commit på story-grenen etter godkjenningen din.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Acceptance audit: Story 1.1 part A against
  `spec-1-1-start-prognose-locally-from-a-fresh-clone.md`

  **Verdict:** the diff violates no acceptance criterion and no frozen
  constraint. One behaviour the spec describes is wrong (finding 1). The other
  four findings are low or informational. I checked the code and tests on the
  final commit (`5268fe0`): 75 passed, `ruff check` printed "All checks
  passed!", and `ruff format --check` printed "20 files already formatted".

  ### Findings

  - **1. A POST to `/` gets Django's English CSRF 403 page, not the blank 405
  the spec and the test describe** (low)
    - **Violates:** the spec's Implementation Notes ("A POST to `/` gets
  Django's blank 405 until Story 1.4 adds the error pages") and the docstring of
  `test_start_page_only_answers_get_and_head` ("AD-10: … a POST gets 405").
  AD-10 is about POST then redirect; it does not set a 405.
    - **Evidence:** `settings.py` enables `CsrfViewMiddleware` (patch line
  1166), and `views.start` uses `@require_safe` (line 1406). The test uses the
  default `client`, which turns CSRF checks off.
    - **My check:** with CSRF checks on,
  `Client(enforce_csrf_checks=True).post("/")` returned 403 with `<html
  lang="en">` ("CSRF verification failed"). With them off, it returned 405 with
  an empty body.
    - **Impact:** a browser or curl sees the English 403. Error pages are Story
  1.4 work, so this is not a scope breach. The Story 1.4 handoff should name the
  CSRF 403 page (AD-12 lists "403 (including a failed CSRF check)").
    - **Fix:** correct the note and the docstring, or add an assertion with CSRF
  checks on that expects 403.

  - **2. 34 of 37 test functions name no FR-n or E-n** (low)
    - **Violates:** context documents only. AD-17 and PRD DR-5 (line 762) both
  say "every test names the FR-n or E-n it verifies", and Story 4.3's check
  (`epics.md`, line 1843) uses the same wording. The frozen spec only says
  "names the IDs it verifies", which these tests meet.
    - **Evidence:** only the three FR-1 tests in `tests/test_company.py` name an
  FR. All the others name only AD or NFR IDs. For example, every test in
  `test_load_seed.py` names `AD-13, NFR-3`, and
  `test_first_migration_creates_company` names `AD-6`.
    - **Why decide now:** Story 1.1 has no FR of its own, so naming NFR and AD
  IDs is defensible. But Story 4.3 would flag these tests as its check is
  written. Either record that NFR and AD IDs count for tests without an FR, or
  reword the 4.3 check, so that 34 docstrings don't need editing later.

  - **3. AC4's "Ruff reads only the Python in `src/` and `tests/`" holds because
  of the current files, not the configuration** (low, informational)
    - **Violates:** the wording of AC4 against review patch P6. The config is
  `include = ["*.py"]` minus `_bmad`, `.claude` and `**/migrations` (patch lines
  837–841). The README says Ruff checks "alle Python-filene i repoet, unntatt
  …" (line 135).
    - **Evidence:** `ruff check --show-files` lists exactly 20 files, all in
  `src/` and `tests/`, so AC4 passes today. Story 1.6's `data/generate_seed.py`
  will also be linted, which the BH-5 triage accepted.
    - **Fix:** reword AC4 to match the P6 decision, so a later auditor doesn't
  read it as broken.

  - **4. The 2026-11-17 QA entry still promises the Windows and offline tests "i
  første story"** (informational)
    - **Conflicts with:** Story 1.1's acceptance criteria, which put the Windows
  and offline tests in Story 1.3.
    - **Evidence:** the unchanged context line "**Gjenstår.** Begge testene
  kjøres i første story …" (patch line 456). The new entry correctly says Story
  1.3, so the log now says both. A short note on the old line, such as "(flyttet
  til story 1.3)", would resolve it.

  - **5. Outside the diff: two branch commits don't name their story**
  (informational)
    - **Conflicts with:** DR-5's "every commit names its story", which Story 4.3
  checks.
    - **Evidence:** `5268fe0` "Fyll inn commit-hashen fa237e7 i endringsloggene
  og QA-loggen" and `79c9154` "Legg til statustabellen og KI-styringsloggen, og
  før inn planleggingsfasen i QA-loggen". Noting this in the pull request may be
  enough. Rewording pushed commits is the user's call.

  ### Checked and conforming
  - **Pins and lock file:** `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` pin Django 5.2.19 and
  psycopg[binary] 3.3.9, and in the `dev` group pytest 9.2.0, pytest-django
  4.14.0, Ruff 0.17.5 and openpyxl 3.1.5. Both say `requires-python ==3.13.*`,
  `.python-version` is 3.13, and `uv.lock` is the only lock file.
  - **`compose.yaml`:** it matches every AD-14 item.
  - **Edge-case matrix:** every row is implemented and tested, including the
  defaults, the phone test, refused hosts, `.env` parsing, the custom URL and
  the three secret-file cases. The DEBUG rule matches the spec.
  - **Error messages:** they are in Norwegian, use `from None`, and never repeat
  the URL or a secret.
  - **Start page:** the sentence on `/` matches the Innlogging wireframe in
  `EXPERIENCE.md` (lines 48–49) word for word.
  - **`.gitignore` and `.env.example`:** `.gitignore` meets Story 1.1's list,
  and the Next.js block, `db.sqlite3*` and `staticfiles/` are gone.
  `.env.example` is not ignored and marks each variable correctly.
  - **Scope:** there is no work from Stories 1.2–1.8, no admin or superuser, no
  raw SQL and no new packages.
  - **Files the patch left out:** `uv.lock`, `data/.gitkeep` and
  `reference/.gitkeep` are on the branch.
  - **Privacy:** there are no local paths, e-mail addresses or secrets in the
  diff or in the branch's other changed files. The session export already masks
  its paths as `/Users/<bruker>`.

  **Scope and method.** I traced the parts that change behaviour: `src/`,

  mutations: your saved scope rule says to ask before working outside the
  project folder, and as the reviewer I should not edit the repo.

  Tests already catch the smallest regression I traced for each of these:
  - `.env` parsing

  ### The P13 fix that forces `prognose.settings` (EC-11) has no regression test

  - **Changed surface:** `src/manage.py:13` always sets
  `os.environ["DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE"] = "prognose.settings"`.
  `pyproject.toml:26` passes `--ds=prognose.settings`. Together they stop an
  exported `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` from another project from winning (review
  pass 1, EC-11 → P13, spec line 122).
  - **Impacted consumer or site:**
    - README steps 3–5 (`uv run python src/manage.py migrate` / `load_seed` /
  `runserver --insecure`) all go through `main()` in `src/manage.py`.
    - `uv run pytest` (README «Tester») depends on `pyproject.toml:26`.
  - **Existing test evidence:**
    - `Regression gap`: I searched `tests/` for `manage`, `subprocess`, `runpy`,
  `sys.executable`, `spec_from_file_location` and `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE`. I
  also searched all project `*.py`/`*.toml`/`*.ini`/`*.cfg` files (excluding
  `.venv`, `_bmad`, `.claude`) for `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` and `--ds`.
    - The only hits are imports of `django.core.management`, the `"manage.py"`
  program-name string in `tests/test_start_command.py:16`, and the settings
  reload at `tests/test_settings.py:59`.
    - No test imports or runs `src/manage.py`, and none exports a foreign
  settings module. `tests/test_start_command.py:11-25` loads runserver
  in-process through `get_commands()`/`load_command_class`, which bypasses
  `manage.py`.
    - There is no conftest, no CI (no `.github`, per D-9) and no active git
  hook.
  - **Missing verification:**
    - An assertion that `main()` leaves `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE ==
  "prognose.settings"` when `annet.settings` is exported.
    - A pytest run with `annet.settings` exported that still starts.
  - **Demonstration:**
    - `Regression gap`: Change line 13 to
  `os.environ.setdefault("DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE", "prognose.settings")`. That
  is Django's stock template, and `src/prognose/wsgi.py:7` in this same diff
  still uses it. Every test still passes, because no test runs `manage.py`.
    - Or replace `addopts = "--ds=prognose.settings"` with the ini key
  `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE = "prognose.settings"`. The spec's own Code Map (line
  59) calls it «pytest `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE`». Every test still passes on any
  machine without an exported variable.
  - **Consequence:** The bug EC-11 reproduced comes back with no signal. With
  another project's `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` exported, README steps 3–5 and `uv
  run pytest` fail with `ImportError`.
  - **Disposition:** `patch`.
    - Add a test in the style `tests/test_settings.py` already uses
  (`importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` plus `monkeypatch`): load
  `src/manage.py`, `monkeypatch.setenv("DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE",
  "annet.settings")`, stub `django.core.management.execute_from_command_line` to
  record its argument, call `main()`, then assert the variable is
  `prognose.settings` and the stub received `sys.argv`.
    - For `--ds`: `subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "pytest",
  "--collect-only", "-q", "tests/test_start_page.py"], cwd=REPO_ROOT,
  env={**os.environ, "DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE": "annet.settings"})` returns 0.

  ### `test_dotenv_values_never_reach_os_environ` stubs out the only code that
  reads `.env`

  - **Changed surface:** The AD-13 rule that reading `.env` never changes
  `os.environ`. It is stated in three places:
    - the spec's frozen «Always» list (line 26)
    - the `src/prognose/settings.py:6` docstring
    - `src/prognose/env.py:3-4`

    The only code that reads `.env` is `env.read_dotenv`
  (`src/prognose/env.py:34-63`), called once at `src/prognose/settings.py:17`.
  - **Impacted consumer or site:** Loading the settings at
  `src/prognose/settings.py:17`, which every `manage.py` command and the pytest
  session do.
  - **Existing test evidence:**
    - `Broken-verification gap`: `tests/test_settings.py:216-224` takes a
  snapshot of `os.environ` around `load_settings(...)`. But the `load_settings`
  fixture replaces `env.read_dotenv` with `stub_read_dotenv`, which returns a
  dict (`tests/test_settings.py:47-49, 55`). The real reader never runs, so the
  test can only catch a write in `settings.py` itself.
    - The tests that run the real reader (`tests/test_settings.py:227-251`,
  `254-256`, and the not-UTF-8 and folder tests after them) assert only the
  returned dict or the error. None of them compares `os.environ`.
  - **Missing verification:** A call to the real `env.read_dotenv` on a `.env`
  in `tmp_path`, followed by an assertion that `dict(os.environ)` is unchanged.
  - **Demonstration:**
    - `Broken-verification gap`: Add `os.environ.setdefault(key, values[key])`
  after `src/prognose/env.py:62`.
    - `test_dotenv_values_never_reach_os_environ` still passes because of the
  stub. `test_dotenv_with_byte_order_mark_comments_and_quotes` still passes
  because the dict it returns is unchanged.
    - The leaked `DATABASE_URL`/`PROGNOSE_*` values also stay invisible to later
  tests, because the fixture deletes those variables before each reload
  (`tests/test_settings.py:42-44`).
  - **Consequence:** The frozen rule breaks silently. Every `.env` value,
  including a `DATABASE_URL` password (`.env.example:13` calls it secret), gets
  copied into `os.environ`. It is then inherited by the child processes of
  `manage.py` commands, such as the child process runserver restarts on code
  changes.
  - **Disposition:** `patch` — in
  `test_dotenv_with_byte_order_mark_comments_and_quotes`, take a snapshot of
  `dict(os.environ)` before the real `env.read_dotenv(path)` call and assert it
  is unchanged afterwards. Its keys `NAVN`, `KORTNAVN` and `TOM` are new to the
  environment, so a write would show.

  ### Nothing checks that git ignores the `.secret_key` that `load_seed` writes
  into the working folder

  - **Changed surface:** The Prognose block in `.gitignore:54-56` was rewritten:
  new comment, and `db.sqlite3*` and `staticfiles/` removed. `.secret_key` at
  line 56 is now its only rule. README.md:49 promises «Git ignorerer fila, så
  nøkkelen kommer aldri inn i repoet».
  - **Impacted consumer or site:**
    - `load_seed` (`src/web/management/commands/load_seed.py:21-25`) creates
  `settings.SECRET_KEY_FILE`, which is `BASE_DIR / ".secret_key"` in the repo
  root (`src/prognose/settings.py:20`), on every fresh setup (README step 4).
    - Secondary: `.env`. Its rule (`.gitignore:27-28`) did not change, but this
  diff makes `.env` a settings input and documents that it can hold a password
  (`.env.example:5, 13`).
  - **Existing test evidence:**
    - `Regression gap`: `tests/` has no hits for `gitignore`, `check-ignore`,
  `check_ignore`, `git ` or `subprocess`.
    - `tests/test_load_seed.py:19-20` points `SECRET_KEY_FILE` at `tmp_path`, so
  no test ever looks at the file in the repo root. There is no CI and no active
  hook.
    - Review pass 1 rejected this as BH-1 («unlikely», spec line 95) and relied
  on the privacy scan before commits, which is a manual/agent step. Against
  that, this same diff records it happening: `docs/qa-logg.md:223` (row 15) says
  the design branch's `.gitignore` lacked `.secret_key` on 16 November.
  - **Missing verification:** `git check-ignore -q` returns 0 for
  `settings.SECRET_KEY_FILE` and for `settings.BASE_DIR / ".env"`.
  - **Demonstration:**
    - `Regression gap`: Delete `.gitignore:56`, or rewrite the block again
  without it, as happened on the design branch. All tests still pass.
    - After README step 4, `git status` lists `.secret_key` as untracked, and
  `git add .` / `git add -A` stages it.
  - **Consequence:** The key that signs sessions (NFR-3) can be committed and
  pushed to GitHub with no automated signal, which breaks the promise in
  README.md:49.
  - **Disposition:** `patch` — add a test in `tests/test_load_seed.py` that runs
  `git check-ignore -q` on both paths with `cwd=settings.BASE_DIR`. It should
  skip outside a git working tree, for example in a copy without `.git`.

  ### The 127.0.0.1-only database port, which makes trust authentication safe,
  is checked only by hand

  - **Changed surface:** In `compose.yaml:11-13`, `POSTGRES_HOST_AUTH_METHOD:
  trust` is safe only because the port is published as `"127.0.0.1:15432:5432"`.
  The file's comment (lines 3-4, NFR-3) says so, and so does the spec's
  acceptance criterion «listens only on `127.0.0.1:15432` and has no password»
  (spec line 72).
  - **Impacted consumer or site:** `docker compose up -d --wait` (README step 1)
  on every machine that runs Prognose, including during the phone test, when
  the machine is on a local network.
  - **Existing test evidence:**
    - `Regression gap`: `tests/` has no hits for `compose`, `host_ip` or
  `POSTGRES`. The only hit for `15432` is the client-side `"PORT": 15432` in
  `test_defaults_need_no_variables_and_no_env_file`
  (`tests/test_settings.py:88`). That pins where the app connects, not where the
  server listens.
    - The database tests connect over 127.0.0.1, which works whichever addresses
  the port is published on.
    - The only check is the manual `lsof` in each QA setup entry
  (`docs/qa-logg.md:109, 319`).
  - **Missing verification:** An automated check that every published port in
  `docker compose config --format json` has `host_ip` set to `127.0.0.1`.
  - **Demonstration:**
    - `Regression gap`: Change the mapping to `"15432:5432"`. Every test still
  passes, because Docker still serves 127.0.0.1:15432 when it publishes on all
  addresses.
  - **Consequence:** A passwordless database, whose `prognose` role is the
  image's superuser, would accept connections from any machine on the network,
  and only a manual `lsof` check would notice.
  - **Disposition:** `defer` — `docker` may not be on PATH when pytest runs
  (spec line 139), so a pytest check would often skip. The check fits better as
  a scripted step next to the QA setup runs, or in the CI that story 1.2 adds.

⏺ Agent "Edge Case Hunter code review 1.1" finished · 15m 18s

› Message from general-purpose
  Finding floor: the diff is 134,747 bytes ≈ 134.7 kB, so N = min(floor(√134.7 +
  1), 10) = min(floor(12.61), 10) = min(12, 10) = **10**.

  - **The 405 test checks a path that real clients never reach.** In
  `<repo>/tests/test_start_page.py`,
  `test_start_page_only_answers_get_and_head` gets 405 only because Django's
  test `Client` skips CSRF checks by default, so the POST reaches
  `require_safe`.
    - A real browser or `curl` POST is stopped first by `CsrfViewMiddleware`. It
  gets 403 and Django's stock CSRF page (`<html lang="en">`, title «403
  Forbidden»).
    - I ran it with `Client(enforce_csrf_checks=True)`: POST, PUT and DELETE
  give 403, OPTIONS gives 405.
    - The spec says the same wrong thing: «A POST to `/` gets Django's blank 405
  until Story 1.4». Story 1.4 needs a failed CSRF check to give 403 «Skjemaet
  er utløpt», and a client that skips CSRF would hide that path.
    - Fix: use a CSRF-enforcing client, assert 403 for POST and 405 for OPTIONS,
  and correct the docstring and the spec sentence.

  - **A `DATABASE_URL` exported for another project silently wins.** In
  `<repo>/src/prognose/settings.py` (through
  `env.config_value`), the environment wins over `.env` and over the Compose
  default.
    - `DATABASE_URL` is a common, generic name. If a shell profile exports one
  for another project, `migrate` creates Prognose's tables in that database.
    - `uv run pytest` then works on that server too. pytest-django calls
  `setup_databases(interactive=False)`, so an existing `test_<name>` there is
  dropped and recreated without asking.
    - Nothing tells the user which database is in use. EC-11 fixed this same
  problem for `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE`. `tests/test_settings.py` strips
  `DATABASE_URL` so its results «never depend on that machine», but the app and
  the real test run are not protected.
    - libpq's own `PG*` variables (for example `PGSSLMODE=require`) get in the
  same way, because Django passes only the values it has.
    - Fix, at minimum: a warning in README and `.env.example`, plus printing the
  effective `host:port/name` (never the password). The alternative is a
  Prognose-specific variable name, which needs an AD-13 amendment.

  - **With no `LOGGING` setting and DEBUG off by default, tracebacks are
  dropped.** This is in
  `<repo>/src/prognose/settings.py`.
    - Django's default `console` handler carries the `require_debug_true`
  filter. `mail_admins` sends nothing without `ADMINS`.
    - So a view exception shows only `"GET / HTTP/1.1" 500` in the runserver
  console, and the traceback appears nowhere.
    - AD-12 requires «Errors are logged to the console…». That is scheduled for
  Story 1.4, but this diff already makes DEBUG off the default, and neither
  README nor `.env.example` says tracebacks need `PROGNOSE_DEBUG=1`.
    - Fix: add a minimal `LOGGING` now (`django.request` errors go to the
  console whatever DEBUG is), or record the gap.

  - **`<repo>/src/prognose/wsgi.py` still uses
  `os.environ.setdefault`.** The EC-11 fix forces `prognose.settings` in
  `manage.py` and pytest, but not here. Any route into `prognose.wsgi` that
  doesn't go through `manage.py` (an IDE run configuration, a WSGI server, a
  script import) still loads a foreign exported `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE`. Fix:
  assign it as `manage.py` does, or add a comment explaining why it differs.

  - **No automated test that the start command listens only on 127.0.0.1.** The
  acceptance criterion says «the documented start command listens only on
  `127.0.0.1` (AD-13)».
  `<repo>/tests/test_start_command.py` checks only
  `--insecure` and the WSGI loading, so only the manual `lsof` check in the QA
  log covers the address. Fix: one assertion on the staticfiles runserver
  command, `default_addr == "127.0.0.1"` and `default_port == "8000"`.

  - **No regression test for the EC-11 fix in
  `<repo>/src/manage.py`.** Nothing tests that
  `manage.py` always uses `prognose.settings`, although the QA log lists it as
  fixed. Fix: a subprocess test running `manage.py check` with
  `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=annet.settings` would pin it without touching the
  database.

  - **The «owner only» claim for `.secret_key` is not true on Windows, a
  supported platform.** The comment in
  `<repo>/src/web/management/commands/load_seed.py`
  says «Only the owner may read or write it».
    - On Windows, the `0o600` passed to `os.open` only sets the read-only flag.
  Access follows the folder's inherited ACLs, so in a folder such as `C:\dev\…`
  other local users can usually read the key.
    - The test skips the check outside POSIX, and nothing in `issues.md` hands
  this to Story 1.3.
    - Fix: qualify the comment and add a check or a note for the Windows test.

  - **The README never says how to stop or remove what it starts.** This covers
  `<repo>/README.md` and `compose.yaml`.
    - Because of `restart: unless-stopped`, the database comes back every time
  Docker Desktop starts until someone runs `docker compose stop`. It keeps port
  15432 busy, which blocks a second clone; the QA log had to stop the repo's
  database for the clone test.
    - The README mentions none of Ctrl+C for `runserver`, `docker compose stop`
  / `down`, or `down -v` to delete the data.
    - BH-3 moved start/stop to Story 4.1 without considering the restart policy.
  A short «Stopp Prognose» section would help anyone who follows «Kom i gang»
  before 4.1.

  - **The AI steering log's build-phase section is out of date after D-9.** In
  `<repo>/docs/ki-styringslogg.md`, «Byggefasen» says
  story 1.1 «ga ingen tilfeller, fordi brukeren godtok hver anbefaling fra
  Claude», citing the part B spec.
    - The QA-log entry «Motsigelse i planen om CI før story 1.2», added in this

      352    - The tests run against the local PostgreSQL, and the app wr
           ites no files at request time.
      353  - **AD-15 Seed data and the explicit reset.** `data/generate_s
           eed.py` writes the versioned JSON files in `data/seed/` and gi
           ves identical output on every run. The files hold inputs and p
           assword hashes made with Django's hasher and a fixed salt per 
           company. The rule test products take their series from the ref
           erence spreadsheet, and the two test companies have disjoint p
           roduct numbers. `load_seed` without options loads into an empt
           y database only; when any company exists, it stops with a Norw
           egian message and changes nothing. `load_seed --reset` is the 
           only command that deletes data: it asks for typed confirmation
           , then, in one transaction, deletes the companies with their d
           ata and all sessions and loads the seed. Nothing runs it autom
           atically.
      354  - **AD-16 Publishing on Vercel (removed from v1).** Removed on
            2026-11-19 together with Story 1.9 (D-1); the spine keeps the
            decision as history.

      2  
      3  KI-styringsloggen oppfyller DR-5 i PRD-en. Den fører tilfeller d
         er gruppen avklarte, avviste eller rettet det KI foreslo. Hvert 
         tilfelle har fem felt: hva KI foreslo, hva som var feil, hva gru
         ppen bestemte, commiten eller pull requesten, og kilden. PRD-en 
         krever minst fem tilfeller, og minst ett for hver epic som blir 
         bygd (D-2 i `epics.md`).
      4

        -r 2026. Den står i `epics.md` (linje 598–604) og gjelder for hve
        -r story:                                                        
      5 +**Rutinen for KI-økter.** Brukeren godkjente rutinen 18. novembe
        +r 2026. Den står i `epics.md` (linje 598–604 i `d8674ad`) og gje
        +lder for hver story:                                            
      6  - En Claude Code-økt for en story begynner med å nevne storyen.
      7  - Når økten er ferdig, eksporteres samtalen med `/export` til `.
         logs/<bruker>/sessions/`, med storyen og datoen i filnavnet. En 
         økt som ikke formet storyens kode, tester eller dokumenter, tren
         ger ikke lagres.
      8  - Før commit får eksporten den samme personvernkontrollen som al
         le commits. Bare treffene fjernes eller maskeres, så promptene f
         orblir originale.

      79  BMAD Code Review of the whole story branch (2026-11-21; PR #8):
           Blind Hunter (BH), Edge Case Hunter (EC), Verification Gap (VG
          ) and Acceptance Auditor (AA), on 32 files from `ae7b72c` to `5

          a Review Triage Log of part A or part B keep that verdict («car
          ried»).

      81 -- [ ] [Review][Decision] Guard the `.gitignore` rules for `.sec
         -ret_key` and `.env` with a test (VG-3; carried BH-1 from review
         - pass 1, rejected then as unlikely) — The rules hold today, but
         - no test fails if a later edit drops them, and the QA log recor
         -ds that the design branch's `.gitignore` lacked `.secret_key` o
         -n 2026-11-16. Options: a `git check-ignore` test that skips out
         -side a git working tree, or keep the earlier rejection.        
      82 -- [ ] [Review][Decision] Tests that verify no FR name only NFR,
         - DR or AD IDs (AA-2) — DR-5 says every test names the FR-n or E
         --n it verifies; 34 of the 37 tests verify no FR and name NFR, D
         -R or AD IDs instead, which Story 4.3's check would flag. Option
         -s: decide now in `epics.md` (D-10) that such tests name their N
         -FR, DR or AD IDs, or leave it to Story 4.3 as an open point.   
      83 -- [ ] [Review][Decision] A `DATABASE_URL` exported for another 
         -project wins (BH-2) — The environment wins over `.env` and the 
         -Compose default, so `migrate` and `uv run pytest` would use the
         - other project's server, where pytest-django drops and recreate
         -s an existing `test_<name>` without asking. Options: a warning 
         -in the README and `.env.example`; a Prognose-specific variable 
         -name, which amends AD-13 and the Configuration convention; or n
         -o change.

         + no test fails if a later edit drops them, and the QA log recor
